| RD-10 | The corner radius of the chart filter toggle lives in a `.chart-filter-toggle` class rule in `frappe/public/scss/desk/desktop.scss`, written as `.btn-group > .btn.chart-filter-toggle:not(:last-child):not(.dropdown-toggle) { border-radius: var(--radius) }` and placed unscoped beside the `.widget` block, replacing the inline `style="border-radius: var(--radius);"` that `frappe.dashboard_utils.render_chart_filters` emitted on the button. | (a) The literal shape `.chart-filter-toggle { border-radius: var(--radius) }`; (b) `.btn-group > .btn.chart-filter-toggle` without the two `:not()` qualifiers; (c) keep the declaration inline; (d) `border-radius: var(--radius) !important` on a single-class selector; (e) add bootstrap's own `dropdown-toggle` class to the button so its corner reset self-exempts; (f) nest the rule inside `.widget` beside the other dashboard widget rules. | Collapsing the toggle into one button made it a direct `.btn` child of `.btn-group` that is never `:last-child`, because its `<ul class="dropdown-menu">` sibling follows it, so bootstrap 4.6.2's reset at specificity (0,4,0) zeroes its right corners: measured in Chrome, the same button computes 8px/0/0/8px without an override and 8px on all four corners with one. (a) at (0,1,0) and (b) at (0,3,0) both lose that cascade and would ship square right corners, and frappe's own `.btn { border-radius: var(--radius) }` loses for the same reason; the chosen selector is (0,5,0) and mirrors the reset it answers. (c) is the finding being resolved. (d) wins by force and hides from either stylesheet the reason the rule exists. (e) makes bootstrap render its own `::after` caret beside the chevrons icon the control already draws. (f) would narrow a declaration that was previously unscoped, and `render_chart_filters` is a public util whose output also renders outside `.widget`. | The selector is coupled to the shape of bootstrap's reset: an upgrade that drops the `:not(:last-child)` qualifier would leave this rule matching nothing and the toggle would silently lose its right corners. If the markup ever renders the button as the last child of the group the rule stops applying, which is harmless because the reset stops applying with it. The class name is now part of the rendered markup, so anything selecting the toggle by its inline style would no longer match; the repository contains no such selector, and the `.chart-actions`, `.timespan-filter`, `.time-interval-filter` and `[data-toggle="dropdown"]` selectors the Cypress spec uses are unchanged. |
| RD-11 | The "focus is unplaced" test of the `hidden.bs.dropdown` handler in `frappe.dashboard_utils.make_dropdown_keyboard_operable` treats `document.documentElement` as unplaced alongside `document.body`, matching the sibling test in `render_chart_filters`, so RD-5's condition reads `!focused \|\| focused === document.body \|\| focused === document.documentElement \|\| $menu[0].contains(focused)` (pipes escaped per RK-4). | (a) Keep `document.body` alone, as RD-5 first implemented it; (b) drop the `documentElement` arm from `render_chart_filters` instead, so the two agree the other way; (c) test `!document.hasFocus() \|\| focused === document.documentElement` and drop the `body` arm. | One file held two tests of the same condition and the narrower of them decided whether a keyboard user got focus back. Chrome and Firefox report `<body>` when nothing is focused, so (a) is correct almost always and fails silently when it is not; (b) would remove a working guard to reach agreement; (c) would change what "unplaced" means for the other call site as well. Verified in Chrome by parking focus on `<html>` with the menu open and closing the menu programmatically: focus returns to the toggle. | The handler now refocuses the toggle in one case where it previously left focus on the document element; that is the intended behaviour and the only behavioural difference. RD-5's row text enumerates the pre-change cases and is read together with this row. |
| RD-12 | `NumberCardWidget` re-homes the keyboard after it rebuilds its own action area: `render_card()` records the control that holds focus by CSS selector before `prepare_actions()` and re-matches that selector in the rebuilt area afterwards, through `action_area_holds_focus()` / `get_focused_control()` / `focus_control()` / `restore_rebuilt_control_focus()` — the chart widget's RB-6 vocabulary, so the two widgets read alike. The `Refresh` command additionally records `.card-menu` itself immediately before calling `render_card()`. The restore is skipped only while focus sits on a connected element outside this widget, and the record is cleared as it is consumed. `frappe.dashboard_utils.make_dropdown_keyboard_operable` and its `hidden.bs.dropdown` handler are left exactly as they are. | (a) Re-resolve the toggle from the live DOM inside the `hidden.bs.dropdown` handler, replacing its `!toggle.isConnected` early exit; (b) keep a reference to the focused node and refocus it after the rebuild; (c) anchor the restore on the toggle's generated id; (d) narrow the `Refresh` command so it rebuilds only the card body and leaves the action area standing; (e) rely on `get_focused_control()` alone and drop the record the command sets; (f) defer the rebuild to a later task so Bootstrap's close runs first. | Measured in Chrome on the running dashboard, twice, with a listener on the `.card-actions` container and again with a document-level delegated listener: on this path `hide.bs.dropdown` and `hidden.bs.dropdown` **never fire on any node**. `set_card_actions()`'s `this.action_area.html(...)` detaches the whole open dropdown inside the same click dispatch, so Bootstrap's `_clearMenus`, which re-queries the document for toggles, never sees it — the detached subtree stays frozen with `show` and `aria-expanded="true"` — which makes (a) dead code on the only path that needs it. The focused item and the toggle are both destroyed and the toggle's id is regenerated on every rebuild (`unique-0` then `unique-6` then `unique-7`, observed), so (b) and (c) have nothing to hold. (d) would narrow a command whose contract is to re-render the card from its document, a larger behavioural change than returning focus. (e) leaves the mouse path broken, which the finding requires: clicking a `.dropdown-item` anchor, which is not focusable, blurs the toggle, so `document.activeElement` is already `body` before the command runs. (f) would make a synchronous command asynchronous and would still leave Bootstrap's own restoration pointing at a toggle the rebuild then discards. | Any `render_card()` that runs while the keyboard is inside the card's action area now re-homes focus onto the rebuilt toggle, which is wider than the `Refresh` command alone — a dashboard-wide refresh behaves the same way; that is the chart widget's behaviour and D6 clause b's intent. A card in customize mode renders no `.card-menu`, so the restore finds no match and is a silent no-op. A future command that both rebuilds the action area and synchronously moves focus to another control inside the same widget would have that move overridden; a command that routes away does not rebuild and keeps using `suppress_menu_focus_restore()` (RD-5). This row corrects RD-5's risk text, which said a widget rebuilding its control row restores focus through the chart widget's path: the Number Card has its own, and neither runs through `hidden.bs.dropdown`. |
| RD-13 | The time-window dropdowns are rendered into one group element that lays them out in reading order: `render_chart_filters` creates `div.chart-filter-group` carrying inline `display: flex; align-items: center; gap: 5px`, inserts it into the container where the individual dropdowns used to go (the `append` argument still choosing prepend or append), and inserts each rendered dropdown at the **start** of that group, so the group's document order is its painted left-to-right order. `render_date_range_field()` places `.dashboard-date-field` as the group's first child instead of after the timespan filter. | (a) Emit the dropdowns in the reverse order, which reverses the painted order with them; (b) set `flex-direction: row` on `.widget-control` in `frappe/public/scss/desk/desktop.scss` and reverse the insertion order of every control in the row; (c) give the two toggles explicit `order` values; (d) positive `tabindex` values on the toggles; (e) `display: contents` on the group; (f) leave `render_date_range_field()`'s insertion point as it was; (g) fix the whole header, chart menu and filter button included. | `.widget .widget-head .widget-control` computes `display: flex; flex-direction: row-reverse`, so painted order is the exact reverse of document order — measured at 1440 px, DOM order `chart-menu` (left 1371), interval (1289), timespan (1194), `filter-chart` (1161), and Shift+Tab visibly moves the ring rightward. That relation holds for any permutation of the DOM, so (a) cannot fix it. (c) cannot either: `order` is resolved against every flex item of the container, while these two toggles must stay between two children whose `order` is `0` and which are rendered by regions other work units hold at this checkpoint, so any non-zero order moves them past both. (e) leaves the children in the same flex container and therefore in the same reversed order, and (d) is an anti-pattern that would distort the rest of the tab order. (b) is the direct fix and belongs to whoever owns `desktop.scss`, which two other work units hold at this checkpoint — hence the group's three declarations inline on the element this helper itself renders. A group laying its own children out left to right decouples the two orders: the painted result is unchanged (measured identical lefts and 5 px gaps) while the keyboard reaches "Last Week" before "Daily". (f) would paint the date-range control between the two toggles, because `insertAfter(".timespan-filter")` means "left of the timespan toggle" in a reversed row and "right of it" inside the group; the group's first place keeps the control painted where it is today and makes it the group's first tab stop too. (g) is out of reach here: the chart menu and the filter button are inserted by `set_chart_actions()` and `setup_filter_button()`, and the container's direction is the stylesheet rule above. | The widget header carries one more element, so anything assuming the dropdowns or the date-range control are direct children of `.widget-control` must use a descendant selector; every selector in the tree already does, and `.widget-control > *` supplies only `margin-left: 5px`, `align-self: center` and `box-shadow: none`, which the group's own margin, `align-items: center`, `gap: 5px` and `.widget .btn { box-shadow: none }` reproduce. The group's layout is inline, so a site cannot restyle its internal spacing from a stylesheet without `!important`. Outside the group the header's tab order is still the reverse of its painted order — `button.chart-menu` first, `button.filter-chart` last — so the two controls the finding names agree while the rest waits on the container's own direction. An empty `filters` list now renders no group, where it previously rendered nothing at all. |
| RD-14 | `ShortcutWidget` activates from the keyboard: the click body moves unchanged into `activate(e)`, and a namespaced `keydown.shortcut` binding runs the same activation for `Enter`, `" "` and the legacy `"Spacebar"`, ignoring events whose target is not the tile itself and doing nothing while the workspace is in customize mode. This is a deliberate deviation from the QA report's own suggested fix, which was to leave the gap and record it in §4. | (a) Record the gap and leave the tiles inoperable, as the report recommends; (b) drop `role="link"` and `tabindex="0"` so the tile stops advertising a control it is not; (c) render the tile as a real `<a href>`; (d) bind the keys at the workspace or document level; (e) an unnamespaced `keydown` binding. | The tile already advertises an operable link — `role="link"`, `tabindex="0"`, an `aria-label` and a tab stop — and the finding's own expected outcome is that Enter activates it, which is WCAG 2.1.1 Keyboard, Level A. Measured on the running Desk: both `/desk/build` tiles carried `["click"]` and nothing else, no key handler existed anywhere in the tile's ancestor chain, and the tile has no `href`, so Enter on the first tile and Space on the second left `location.pathname`, `location.search`, `frappe.get_route()`, `document.title` and the focus position byte-identical and silent, while a mouse click on the same tile navigated to `/desk/doctype/view/list`. (a) would leave a Level A failure standing on a surface whose sibling control, the Number Card tile, this refinement makes operable, for ten lines in one file. (b) would remove the keyboard reachability and the announced role the workspace deliberately gives these tiles. (c) is the better long-term shape but rewrites the widget's markup, its click semantics and its customize-mode behaviour, far past this finding. (d) would claim keys for every element on the page. (e) would stack one handler per render, which the namespaced binding cannot. | Every workspace shortcut tile now navigates on Enter and Space, new behaviour on a surface no directive named; it is what the tile's role already promises and it matches `NumberCardWidget`. Space is consumed while a tile holds focus, so it no longer scrolls the page from there. The pre-existing unnamespaced `click` binding is untouched, so a re-render still stacks click handlers exactly as before. No Cypress spec visits `/desk/build`, so this fix is verified by hand (§4.4) rather than by an assertion. A focused tile still paints no ring on the tile box — only its label underlines — which is a focus-indicator concern in `frappe/public/scss/desk/desktop.scss`, a file other work units hold at this checkpoint, and is recorded rather than changed. |
| RD-15 | The `div.chart-filter-group` that `render_chart_filters` builds (RD-13) is a wrapping flex row: `flex-wrap: wrap` and `min-width: 0` join its `display: flex; align-items: center; gap: 5px`, so where the widget control row is too narrow to hold the date range control and the two toggles side by side, the group shrinks to the row and lays its members out on further rows in the same left-to-right order. | (a) Leave the group a single non-wrapping row and let it overflow the control row; (b) drop the group and place the toggles and date field directly in `.widget-control`, which RB-12 makes a wrapping row, and fix the tab order some other way; (c) give the group `overflow-x: auto` so its members scroll horizontally; (d) move the layout into `desktop.scss` as a `.widget-control .chart-filter-group` rule instead of the inline style RD-13 chose. | RD-13 groups the timespan and interval toggles and the date range control so document order equals painted order inside a `row-reverse` control row, and RB-12 makes that control row wrap so every control stays inside the card at 375 px. Together, the group became the one child of that row that could not wrap: on a Half-width timeseries widget showing Select Date Range at 375 px it measured about 396 px (220 px date field, two toggles, gaps) inside a 303 px row, and because the row is `row-reverse` the excess ran off the widget's left edge, where the date input's centre was outside the viewport and `elementFromPoint` returned null — the reachability defect R2-F03 that RB-12 fixed, back through another path. Wrapping the group keeps both decisions: its members stay in reading order row by row, and every one of them stays inside the card. (a) is that defect. (b) undoes RD-13's fix for the tab order. (c) would give a 28 px toggle a scrollbar and hide the controls it scrolls to. (d) is equivalent in effect and was not chosen only to keep the group's layout in the one place RD-13 put it, next to the code that builds the group. | A group that wraps takes two rows where one no longer fits, so a narrow header grows by one control row (about 33 px) rather than overflowing; `.widget-control`'s `row-gap: 6px` (RB-12) and the group's own 5 px gap space those rows. The inline style is set through jQuery `.css()`, so a stylesheet cannot override it without `!important`; that is unchanged from RD-13. |

| RE-1 | Keyboard tooltip navigation is a focus-scoped `keydown` handler on the chart widget's plot area; frappe-charts' own `isNavigable` option is not used and `frappe.utils.make_chart` is left unchanged. | (a) Pass `isNavigable: 1` through `make_chart`; (b) bind a document-level `keydown` in the widget and filter by which chart is on screen. | `isNavigable` binds a listener on `document` that acts on key codes 13/37/38/39/40 whenever the chart container is fully inside the viewport, so it would fire for whichever chart happens to be in view and would swallow the arrow keys the widget's own dropdowns rely on; it also drives frappe-charts' overlay/`data-select` mechanism rather than the tooltip the reviewer asked to reach. A handler on the focused element only reacts while that chart has focus, and reuses the tooltip the pointer already shows. | The tooltip is reachable only after focusing the plot area, which adds one tab stop per chart; charts without an x-axis (Pie, Donut, Percentage, Heatmap) get no keyboard tooltip because they expose no `mapTooltipXPosition`. |
| RE-2 | The focusable element is the widget-owned wrapper `div` (`this.chart_wrapper`, marked `.chart-plot-area`), not the `.chart-container` frappe-charts creates inside it. | (a) `.chart-container`; (b) `svg.frappe-chart`. | The wrapper survives both `dashboard_chart.update(data)` and the full chart re-creation the widget performs for circular charts and on "Refresh" — frappe-charts clears the parent's `innerHTML` and builds a fresh `.chart-container` — and the widget creates the wrapper itself, so the ARIA attributes and handlers live where the widget controls the lifecycle. Because `setup_container()` builds a new wrapper on every `make_chart()`, the once-per-element binding guard cannot accumulate listeners. | The focus ring outlines the wrapper, which is marginally larger than the chart; a future frappe-charts version could insert additional nodes between wrapper and chart. |
| RE-3 | The existing mouse `bind_plot_area_tooltip()` stays gated on `valuesOverPoints` and is not extended to other charts. | (a) Bind the widget's own `mousemove` for every axis chart; (b) drop the widget binding and rely entirely on the library. | frappe-charts' `AxisChart.bindTooltip()` already maps any pointer position inside the plot band to the nearest data point when `valuesOverPoints` is falsy — confirmed at runtime on the Line trend chart — and narrows to `.data-point-value` targets only when values are printed over points, which is exactly the gap the widget binding fills. Extending it would duplicate the library's handler on line charts. | A future frappe-charts release that changes `bindTooltip` would leave non-`valuesOverPoints` charts without a plot-area mouse tooltip; the keyboard path is unaffected because it calls `mapTooltipXPosition` directly. |
| RE-4 | Key map: <kbd>→</kbd>/<kbd>←</kbd> step one data point and clamp at the ends, <kbd>Home</kbd>/<kbd>End</kbd> jump to the first/last point, <kbd>Enter</kbd>/<kbd>Space</kbd> re-show the current point, <kbd>Esc</kbd> hides; `preventDefault()` is called only for those keys. | (a) Wrap around at the ends; (b) use <kbd>↑</kbd>/<kbd>↓</kbd> for stepping as `isNavigable` does; (c) make <kbd>Enter</kbd> drill through to the document list. | Clamping pairs with <kbd>Home</kbd>/<kbd>End</kbd> and keeps the ends of the axis discoverable, whereas wrapping silently moves a keyboard user from the last day to the first with no signal. Leaving <kbd>↑</kbd>/<kbd>↓</kbd> and <kbd>Tab</kbd> untouched keeps page scrolling and the widget dropdowns working, which the reviewer's menu directive depends on. Drill-through is a separate behaviour already offered by the chart menu. | Walking a long axis takes one press per point (<kbd>End</kbd> mitigates); users who expect wrap-around get no movement at the ends. |
| RE-5 | The plot area is `role="group"` with an `aria-label` that names the chart and the interaction; no `aria-roledescription` is set. | (a) `role="img"`; (b) `role="application"`; (c) `role="group"` plus `aria-roledescription="chart"`. | `role="img"` declares the subtree a single static graphic, which contradicts a focusable element whose arrow keys change what is announced; `role="application"` suppresses the reader's own navigation for everything inside. `group` keeps the plot area a labelled composite while the live region carries the changing content, and the label already contains the word "chart", so `aria-roledescription` would only add verbosity on the readers that support it. | "group" conveys less about the content type than a chart-specific role would; the label is the only thing telling the user what the arrow keys do. |
| RE-6 | One visually hidden `aria-live="polite" aria-atomic="true"` node per chart widget, reused for every move, holding "<title>: <series> <value>, …"; cleared on <kbd>Esc</kbd> and on blur. | (a) `aria-live="assertive"`; (b) make the library's `.graph-svg-tip` itself the live region; (c) one shared live region for the whole dashboard page. | `polite` lets the reader finish the phrase in progress instead of interrupting on every arrow press, and `atomic` makes it read the whole data point rather than only the changed part. The tooltip node is owned, positioned and hidden by frappe-charts (it only toggles opacity, so stale text stays in the DOM) and its title is upper-cased by CSS, so mirroring the content into a node the widget owns keeps the announcement exact; one region per widget stops two charts from overwriting each other's announcement. | Rapid arrow sequences may be coalesced or queued by the reader; the announcement text duplicates the tooltip, so any future wording change must be made in both places. |
| RE-7 | The announcement text is built from `chart.dataByIndex[index]` (the structure frappe-charts fills the tooltip from) rather than read back out of the tooltip DOM. | Scrape `.title`, `.tooltip-value` and `.tooltip-label` from `.graph-svg-tip` after showing it. | `dataByIndex` holds the same label and the same `formatted` value the tooltip prints, so the spoken and visual content cannot drift, and the announcement does not depend on the tooltip's markup or on the CSS `text-transform` applied to its title. | Couples the widget to a frappe-charts internal — the same coupling the delivered mouse binding already has through `mapTooltipXPosition`. |
| RE-8 | No stylesheet change was made for the plot area by this unit. The plot area nevertheless receives the Desk focus token rather than the browser's native ring, because the contrast work's `.widget [tabindex]:focus-visible` block in `frappe/public/scss/desk/desktop.scss` matches it (measured 7.814:1 light / 6.293:1 dark) — this row's original "relies on the browser's native focus ring" description is superseded by that rule, and rows RF-1, RF-2 and RF-13 hold the ring decisions. | Add a `.chart-plot-area:focus-visible { box-shadow: var(--focus-default); }` rule to `frappe/public/scss/desk/desktop.scss`. | The Desk's global `outline: 0` reset applies only to `a` and `.btn`, so a focusable `div` keeps a visible native focus ring — verified in the browser at 1440px and at 375px — and the Desk stylesheet is being changed by the contrast work in this same refinement, so adding a competing rule here would collide with it. | The plot area's ring is therefore owned by the `desktop.scss` contrast block rather than by this unit: a future change to that block's selector list changes the plot area's indicator with it, and this unit's code carries no ring declaration to notice. |
| RE-9 | The keyboard-tooltip Cypress case seeds two ToDos through the spec's `seed_doc` registry — one allocated to `Administrator`, one to `Cypress.config("testUser")` — so the single-series Top Owners axis holds at least two data points, and reveals the first tooltip through `reveal_first_point(title)`: it first retries until the chart's `svg.frappe-chart` holds no `animate`/`animateTransform` node, focuses the plot area, and then, inside one retried `should`, dispatches a native `keydown` on the plot area (ArrowRight on the first attempt, Home on every later one, each carrying its key code) until `.graph-svg-tip` reports `opacity: 1` with text matching that chart's own series (`Created\|Completed`, `Open ToDos`). No fixed `cy.wait(ms)` remains in the spec. | (a) One fixed `cy.wait` sized to the entry animation; (b) wait on a frappe-charts animation-completion event or promise; (c) disable or stub the library's animation for the test; (d) add a readiness event to the widget for the test to wait on; (e) assert on the second data point only and never touch the first; (f) re-press the key up to ten times at a fixed 500 ms interval, which an earlier draft of this case did. | frappe-charts renders placeholder series while its entry animation runs, so the first keyboard move can land on a point that does not exist yet and read an empty or placeholder tooltip. The library exposes no completion event or promise, which removes (b), and no public switch to turn the animation off for one chart, which leaves (c) as a patch to a vendored library for a test's benefit. (d) would add production code whose only consumer is the spec, against the refinement's minimal-change constraint. A fixed wait (a) and a fixed-interval re-press (f) are the flakiness this replaces — too short on a loaded host, wasted time on an idle one — whereas waiting on the SMIL nodes the library itself removes when the animation ends, and then retrying against the chart's own rendered state, stops as soon as the chart is ready and is bounded by Cypress's command timeout rather than by a sleep count. Seeding two owners is what makes movement along the Top Owners axis observable at all: with one bar, ArrowRight has nowhere to go and the assertion would be about the clamp rather than about the move. | A chart that never settles fails at the Cypress command timeout with a tooltip-content mismatch rather than a timeout message, which reads as a data failure. The polled predicate matches the series names, so renaming a dataset breaks the reveal rather than the assertion that follows it. The two seeded ToDos are ordinary records; the spec's `afterEach` deletes every document `seed_doc` registered, newest first, and asserts each deletion returned `ok`, so a run that stops before the hook — or a deletion the server refuses — is the only way they persist. |
| RE-10 | <kbd>Esc</kbd> on the plot area is contained there: the handler calls `preventDefault()` and `stopPropagation()`, hides the tooltip, clears the live region and returns with focus still on the plot area. No other key the handler consumes stops propagation. | (a) `preventDefault()` only, which is what the first implementation did; (b) let the key propagate and re-focus the plot area afterwards; (c) change the Desk's global Escape handling in `frappe/public/js/frappe/ui/keyboard.js` so it does not blur a focused plot area; (d) stop propagation for every key the plot-area handler consumes. | The Desk's global handler is a bubbling `keydown` on `window` whose Escape branch calls `document.activeElement?.blur()` unconditionally, so Escape dismissed the tooltip and then dropped focus from the plot area to `body`, losing the keyboard user's place in the chart. (b) races the same key press against the blur; (c) changes shared keyboard behaviour for every focusable element in the Desk, which this refinement does not own; (d) would suppress document-level handling for keys that do not conflict — arrows, <kbd>Home</kbd> and <kbd>End</kbd> have no global binding and the global <kbd>Enter</kbd> handler acts only while a confirm dialog is open — so the suppression is kept to the one key that demonstrably conflicts. | An ancestor or document-level Escape listener no longer sees an Escape pressed while a chart plot area holds focus: there, Escape dismisses the tooltip and does nothing else. This matches RD-4, which keeps one owner per key inside a widget. |
| RE-11 | Every fragment of the announcement — the point title, each dataset title and each formatted or raw value — is passed through one helper that stringifies it, strips markup with `strip_html`, decodes HTML entities with `frappe.utils.unescape_html` and trims; a fragment that normalises to an empty string is left out of the announcement. | (a) Insert the `dataByIndex` values unchanged, which announced `Sales &amp;amp; Returns` for a series named `Sales & Returns`; (b) decode through `frappe.utils.html2text`/`DOMParser`; (c) decode entities without stripping markup; (d) read the rendered `.graph-svg-tip` text back out of the DOM (already rejected in RE-7); (e) decode the dataset title only and leave labels and values as they are. | frappe-charts escapes `<`, `>` and `&` in dataset names before storing them in `dataByIndex` (`AxisChart.calcDatasetPoints`) and renders its tooltip through `innerHTML`, so the visible tooltip showed the original characters while the live region — written with jQuery `.text()` — received the entity text. Stripping and then decoding makes the spoken text equal the seen text for all three fragment kinds. (b) parses every fragment through the DOM on each arrow key for the same result on the entities frappe-charts writes; (c) would leave markup produced by a chart's own `formatTooltipX`/`formatTooltipY` formatter in the announcement; (e) leaves the label and value paths inconsistent with the title path. | `unescape_html` decodes the framework's own entity set (`&amp;amp; &amp;lt; &amp;gt; &amp;quot; &amp;#39; &amp;#x60; &amp;#x3D;`), so a numeric entity outside it is announced as written. The order is load-bearing: stripping first and decoding second means a name whose escaped form reads like a tag (`&amp;lt;b&amp;gt;`) is announced literally, which is what the tooltip shows. The announcement is inserted with `.text()`, so decoding cannot put markup into the page. |
| RE-12 | No tooltip path reads frappe-charts' entry placeholder. `chart_data_is_settled()` calls a rendered chart settled once its x axis has labels, every `state.datasets[].values` holds one value per label, and — when the widget has data of its own — that label count equals `this.data.labels.length`; `gate_tooltip_on_settled_data()` wraps `mapTooltipXPosition` once per chart so the widget's keyboard handler, its own plot-area pointer handler and frappe-charts' own pointer handler are all held off until then. A keyboard move made inside that window keeps its index, empties the live region and is re-issued by `await_settled_chart_data()` (50 ms poll, 2 s deadline) the moment the data lands, so the user's own key press produces the real value without a second press. `next_tooltip_request()` tags each move, and a newer move, <kbd>Esc</kbd>, blur, a pointer takeover, a chart this widget has redrawn or discarded and a widget that has left the document each drop a pending one. | (a) Pass `disableEntryAnimation: 1` or `animate: 0` through `get_chart_args`, the fix the review suggested; (b) arm the plot area — `tabindex`, `role`, `aria-label` — only after the chart's `initTimeout`; (c) leave the tooltip alone and suppress only the announcement; (d) force the swap by calling `chart.update(this.data)` at construction or at the first key press; (e) detect the placeholder by its empty dataset names; (f) show the placeholder and re-render on a fixed 700 ms `setTimeout`. | Measured in the vendored build: `BaseChart`'s constructor sets `this.data = this.prepareFirstData(this.realData)` with `initTimeout = 700`, `AxisChart.prepareFirstData` returns the labels minus their last entry and datasets whose names are empty and whose values are zeros, and `update(data, init)` chooses its animation flag as `init ? !disableEntryAnimation : animate` — so (a) decides only whether the 700 ms swap animates and leaves the placeholder window exactly as it was. That is why the suggested fix was not taken and this row exists. (b) would add a tab stop 700 ms into the page, moving the tab order under a user who is already in it. (c) leaves the same key press printing `0` on screen. (d) reaches into the library's entry animation: the vendor's own timer still fires `update(realData, true)` at 700 ms, so an early forced update runs a second cycle over the first while the SMIL swap has the `<svg>` node replaced and restored on a 250 ms and 400 ms pair of timers. (e) is not exclusive to the placeholder — the Group By engine emits datasets with no name at all — while the value count is: `prepareData` pads or truncates every dataset to the label count, so a settled chart always matches and the placeholder never does. (f) is the fixed sleep this replaces, and still shows the wrong value first. Gating the one function every path calls, and re-issuing the move the user actually made, keeps what is shown and what is spoken equal to the chart's data at every instant. | For the first ~700 ms of a chart's life no tooltip appears at all, for the pointer as well as the keyboard: a hover in that window now shows nothing where it used to show zeros. A keyboard move made in that window is honoured up to ~700 ms later, and after the 2 s deadline it is dropped and the next key press retries. The settled test reads two frappe-charts internals (`state.xAxis.labels`, `state.datasets[].values`) — the coupling `mapTooltipXPosition` and `dataByIndex` already carry (RE-7) — so a future release that padded its placeholder values to the full label count would defeat the test and the tooltip would read the placeholder again rather than break. The gate is per chart instance, so a chart the widget re-creates is wrapped again and a discarded one keeps its wrapper until it is collected. |
| RE-13 | `bind_tooltip_aria_state()` wraps the rendered chart's `tip.showTip` and `tip.hideTip` once per tooltip instance, so `.graph-svg-tip` carries `aria-hidden="true"` while frappe-charts holds it at `opacity: 0` and loses the attribute while it is on screen; the initial state is seeded from the tooltip's own opacity at bind time. | (a) Set the attribute from the widget's own keyboard show and hide paths only; (b) empty the tooltip's text when it is hidden; (c) mark the tooltip `aria-hidden` permanently and let the live region carry the keyboard path; (d) move `.graph-svg-tip` out of the plot-area group in the DOM; (e) watch the node's `style` attribute with a `MutationObserver`; (f) leave it as it is. | The library hides its tooltip by setting `opacity: 0` and leaves the last point's text in the DOM, and RE-2 made the wrapper that holds it a focusable `role="group"` — so browse-mode navigation inside the group reached figures from a tooltip the user had dismissed. (a) covers <kbd>Esc</kbd> and blur but not the pointer path or the library's own `mouseleave` handler, which would leave the attribute set on a tooltip that is visible on screen. (b) fights the library, which re-fills the node on the next show. (c) hides a tooltip that is genuinely displayed even where it is the only place the value is written. (d) would reparent a node the library creates inside its own `container` and positions absolutely against it. (e) is a second mechanism to keep in step with an attribute these two functions already fence exactly. Wrapping both puts the attribute where the library itself decides visibility, for every caller. | The wrap is applied to the tooltip object the rendered chart owns; a future library version that replaced its tooltip instance without the widget re-rendering would leave the new one unwrapped, and the widget re-binds on every render. The hidden tooltip keeps its text in the DOM — this hides it from assistive technology rather than clearing it, so anything reading `.graph-svg-tip` text still sees the last point, which is what the resume behaviour in RE-4 depends on. |
| RE-14 | The pointer entering a plot area empties that widget's live region: `release_tooltip_to_pointer()`, bound to `mouseenter` and `mousemove` on the plot area, clears the announcer and drops a keyboard move still waiting on the chart's data, and returns immediately when nothing is announced and nothing is pending. The retained tooltip index is left alone, so a later arrow key still resumes from it. | (a) Keep writing the announcer from the pointer path so it tracks the mouse tooltip; (b) clear it from frappe-charts' own mousemove handler instead; (c) clear it on `mouseleave`; (d) defeat the reader's de-duplication by appending a zero-width character to each announcement or by toggling `aria-live`; (e) leave it as it is. | The region was written by the keyboard path alone, so once the pointer moved the visible tooltip it kept a figure that was no longer on screen; and because `aria-live` does not re-announce identical content, a later keyboard return to that same point was silent. (a) would speak on every pointer movement across the plot area, which is not what a `polite` region is for and not the audience it serves. (b) puts widget behaviour inside a library handler the widget does not own. (c) leaves the contradiction standing for as long as the pointer is inside the chart, which is when it matters. (d) makes every announcement carry an invisible character to work around a reader behaviour that is correct. Clearing on entry is the smallest change that leaves the region either empty or equal to what is displayed; binding `mousemove` as well as `mouseenter` catches a pointer already inside the plot area when the chart renders, and the early return keeps the repeated `mousemove` free of work. | A keyboard user who nudges the mouse loses the current announcement and presses a key to hear it again; the visible tooltip is unaffected. A keyboard move deferred by RE-12 is cancelled by a pointer entering the same plot area, so the tooltip it would have shown does not appear and the next key press re-issues it. |
| RF-1 | Override `--focus-default` and `--focus-outline-default` as scoped custom properties on `.dashboard-page, .widget-group, .widget` (and a dark-theme variant) in `desktop.scss`, rather than raising the token globally. | (a) Change `--focus-default` in `frappe/public/css/espresso/effects.css` globally; (b) write a literal `box-shadow` on each widget control and set no token. | `grep` found 13 consumers of `--focus-default` — `global.scss` (`a`, `.btn`, `.btn-reset:focus-visible`), `buttons.scss` (`.btn:active`, `.btn-secondary`, `.btn-ghost`, `.btn-default`), `filters.scss`, `frappe_datatable.scss`, `checkbox.scss`, `css_variables.scss`, `dark.scss` — which paint the focus ring of every button, link, checkbox, datatable cell and filter on list views, form views, the navbar and modals. D7 forbids changing a token other pages rely on without confirmation, so (a) is out. (b) would leave the token inconsistent with the ring actually painted and would miss controls added later. | The override applies to every Desk surface that renders widgets, not only the dashboard — Workspaces included. That is inside the directive's "Desk dashboard/widget surfaces" scope, and it is the reason the regression check covers list and form views rather than Workspaces. |
| RF-2 | Use `var(--ink-gray-6)` as the ring colour and keep the existing geometry (2px light, 3px dark). | (a) `var(--ink-gray-5)`; (b) `var(--outline-gray-4)`, which the theme cards already use for focus; (c) a literal hex per theme; (d) a single geometry for both themes. | `--ink-gray-6` is one existing design-system token that satisfies AA in both themes with margin: 7.81:1 against `--card-bg` and 7.04:1 against `--control-bg` in light (`#525252`), 6.29:1 and 5.11:1 in dark (`#999999`), against a 3:1 requirement. `--ink-gray-5` reaches only 3.76:1 against `--control-bg` in light and 3.39:1 in dark; `--outline-gray-4` is `#999999` in light, which is 2.85:1 against white and fails. (c) would hardcode colour outside the token system. Keeping the geometry means the only visible change is the hue. | The ring is visually heavier than the near-white original. That is the intended outcome of the directive, but it is a deliberate departure from the shipped visual style. |
| RF-3 | Fix the input placeholder with an explicit `.widget ::placeholder` rule **and** a scoped `--placeholder-color` override, rather than the token alone. | (a) Override `--placeholder-color` only; (b) override `--gray-500` inside the widget scope; (c) change `$input-placeholder-color` in `frappe/public/scss/desk/variables.scss`. | The rule that actually paints the placeholder is bootstrap's compiled `.form-control::placeholder { color: var(--gray-500) }`, built from `$input-placeholder-color`, so (a) changes nothing observable — `--placeholder-color` has no consumer selector at all. (b) would repaint every other use of `--gray-500` inside a widget. (c) is a global change affecting every input in the Desk. The token is still set so the documented placeholder token and the painted colour agree on these surfaces. | Placeholder text now resolves to the same value as `--text-muted`, so a placeholder and muted body text look alike; the typed value stays `--text-color` and remains distinguishable. |
| RF-4 | Raise the chart loading / "No Data" text with `.widget .text-extra-muted { color: var(--ink-gray-6) !important }`. | (a) Raise `--ink-gray-5` globally; (b) change `.text-extra-muted` in `global.scss`; (c) target only `.chart-loading-state.text-extra-muted`. | This is the surface behind the review's "placeholder text at 3.93:1": `global.scss` line 637 sets `.text-extra-muted { color: var(--ink-gray-5) !important }`, and `--ink-gray-5` on `--subtle-accent` measures exactly 3.930:1. `--ink-gray-5` has 44 consumers across the breadcrumb, sidebar, menu, calendar, controls, six espresso component stylesheets, five JS files and the login, signup and update-password pages, so (a) is out under D7. (b) is a global class change. `!important` is required because the rule being overridden carries it. (c) would leave other extra-muted text inside widgets failing. | The `!important` is scoped to `.widget`; a regression probe confirmed `.text-extra-muted` still resolves to `--ink-gray-5` on list and form views. |
| RF-5 | Set `.dashboard-widget-box .chart-loading-state.text-danger { color: var(--ink-red-8) !important }`. | (a) `var(--ink-red-7)`; (b) a per-theme literal hex; (c) change `$danger` in `frappe/public/scss/espresso/_colors.scss`; (d) omit `!important`. | `--ink-red-8` is the one red token that satisfies AA in both themes: light `#b41d1d` gives 6.31:1 on `--subtle-accent`, dark `#ff7575` gives 6.32:1. `--ink-red-7` reaches only 4.45:1 in dark, just under the threshold. (c) would repaint every danger colour in the Desk. (d) was tried first and is dead code: bootstrap's `text-emphasis-variant` mixin emits `.text-danger { color: #e03636 !important }`, which outranks any non-important rule regardless of specificity — the first runtime measurement caught the error text still painting `rgb(224, 54, 54)`. | Chart error text is a noticeably darker red in light mode and a lighter red in dark mode than `.text-danger` elsewhere in the Desk, so the danger colour is not uniform across surfaces. |
| RF-6 | Scope the breadcrumb fix with `body[data-route^="dashboard-view"] .navbar-breadcrumbs a`, using the `data-route` attribute `frappe/public/js/frappe/views/container.js` sets on `body`. | (a) Raise `var(--ink-gray-5)` to `--ink-gray-6` in `frappe/public/scss/desk/breadcrumb.scss` for all routes; (b) raise `--ink-gray-5` globally; (c) scope by a class on the dashboard container. | Breadcrumbs live in the navbar, outside any `.widget`, so a widget-scoped selector cannot reach them; the route attribute is the only hook that limits the change to the Desk dashboard surface the directive names. (a) and (b) would change the breadcrumb colour on every Desk page and, for (b), 44 other consumers — neither is confirmed by the grep, so D7 rules them out. (c) fails because the Desk `dashboard-view` page renders `<div class="dashboard">`; `.dashboard-page` belongs to the separate Workspace dashboard view. | Breadcrumbs remain at 4.17:1 on every other Desk route. Raising them everywhere is a reasonable follow-up but needs the confirmation D7 requires, and the review scoped this work to the dashboard. |
| RF-7 | Raise the breadcrumb `/` separator (`a:before`) to `var(--ink-gray-6)`, the same token the breadcrumb link itself uses, so the glyph clears the 4.5:1 text bar and not only the 3:1 non-text floor. | (a) Leave it at the shipped `--ink-gray-4` (2.85:1 light); (b) raise it to `--ink-gray-5` (4.174:1 light, 4.177:1 dark) and hold the glyph to 3:1 by treating it as decoration; (c) keep `--ink-gray-5` and record the decorative reading in this table instead of changing the colour; (d) a literal hex between the two tokens. | D7 asks for 4.5:1 on text and names "breadcrumbs at 4.17:1" as one of its four known failures, and `--ink-gray-5` resolves to exactly 4.174:1 light / 4.177:1 dark — the figure under repair — so (b) and (c) leave the separator on it and rest on a WCAG 1.4.3 decoration reading that the rendered page contradicts: the `::before` sits inside the anchor and Chrome exposes it in the accessible name, announcing the crumb as "/ ToDo Analytics". `--ink-gray-6` is the link's own token, so no new value enters the design system and one token covers both themes — measured in the running Desk at 7.814:1 light (#525252 on #ffffff) and 6.293:1 dark (#999999 on #171717). The hierarchy cost that pointed at `--ink-gray-5` does not arise, because the only separator that paints is the one before the current-page crumb and `.navbar-breadcrumbs li:last-child > a` holds that crumb at `--ink-gray-9` with `!important` (#0f0f0f light, #f8f8f8 dark), so the separator reads as an intermediate crumb's tone and stays subordinate to the emphasised one. (d) would hardcode a colour outside the token system. | The separator is now the same tone as an intermediate crumb's link text rather than a lighter tick, so on a trail of three or more crumbs the separators carry the same weight as the links they sit between. |
| RF-8 | In the Cypress assertion, read the focus-ring colour from the focused control's computed `box-shadow` when it carries a colour, and otherwise from its resolved `--focus-default`. | (a) Assert only on the computed `box-shadow`; (b) assert only on the token; (c) skip the focus-ring assertion and rely on the runtime screenshots. | Chrome matches `:focus-visible` on a programmatic focus only when the last input modality was the keyboard, and the synthetic clicks a Cypress run performs set the pointer modality, so (a) is flaky — it can read `none` and fail for a reason unrelated to contrast. The token is the exact value the browser paints when the ring does appear, so the fallback keeps the assertion meaningful. (c) would leave the fix without automated coverage, which D10.6 asks for. | The assertion can pass on the token while the painted ring is suppressed by an unrelated cascade change. The painted ring is therefore also verified by pixel sampling in the runtime evidence, and listed as a manual check in section 4. |
| RF-9 | Implement WCAG 2.1 contrast inside the Cypress case rather than importing it: a small CSS colour parser (`#rgb`, `#rgba`, `#rrggbb`, `#rrggbbaa`, `rgb()`, `rgba()`, and the first colour out of a compound value such as a `box-shadow`), alpha compositing of a translucent foreground over its background, the sRGB relative-luminance formula with the 0.03928 knee and the 0.2126 / 0.7152 / 0.0722 coefficients, the `(L1+0.05)/(L2+0.05)` ratio, and an ancestor walk that takes the first ancestor painting a background other than `transparent` / `rgba(0, 0, 0, 0)` as the effective background, defaulting to white. Every input is read from `getComputedStyle` in the running Desk. | (a) Add `cypress-axe`/`axe-core` or a contrast npm package; (b) assert the resolved token values against a table of ratios measured once by hand; (c) compare screenshots or sample pixels; (d) leave the ratios to the manual measurement recorded in §7. | A dependency (a) is ruled out by D1: `package.json` and `yarn.lock` must stay byte-identical, CI asserts the lockfile is unchanged, and `run-ui-tests` installs Cypress itself with `--no-lockfile` — and an axe audit would report a whole-page result rather than the named surfaces the directive lists. (b) proves the stylesheet rather than the rendered colour, and the defect this unit actually fixed (RF-5) was a rule that lost the cascade to a bootstrap `!important`, which a token table would have scored as passing. (c) yields a difference, not a ratio, and is the least stable thing available in a headless run. Computing from computed styles is the only form that measures what the browser paints at the element the requirement is about, and compositing is required because the shipped ring is a translucent `rgba()` over an opaque card — the pre-refine `#c9c9c9e5` — which an uncomposited ratio misreports. | The parser covers the forms the Desk resolves to and returns `null` for `hsl()`, `color-mix()`, `color(display-p3 …)` or a named colour, so such a value fails the assertion for being unreadable rather than for failing contrast. Taking the first colour of a compound `box-shadow` assumes the ring's colour leads the value; RF-8's token fallback covers the case where no colour is present at all. The background walk stops at the first painted ancestor, so a background image, a gradient or a stack of translucent ancestors is not modelled, and nothing painted anywhere resolves to white. The maths is a second implementation of the §7 measurement, so the two have to stay in step. |
| RF-10 | The D7 Cypress case measures both shipped themes in one `it`: the probe set is one routine called twice, first under an explicit `frappe.ui.set_theme("light")` pass and then under a `frappe.ui.set_theme("dark")` pass. Each pass additionally asserts the scoped focus-ring geometry that theme declares (`--focus-default` containing `0px 0px 0px 2px` and `--focus-outline-default` containing `2px solid` in light, `3px` in dark), every assertion message is prefixed with the theme, a guard asserts the page background the two passes measured against actually differs, and the theme the spec found active is restored at the end. | (a) Leave the case measuring the active theme only and state in section 4.2 that the dark theme was verified by static computation; (b) a second `it` duplicating the probes for dark; (c) switch themes through the Desk `ThemeSwitcher` dialog or its `toggle_theme`; (d) write `data-theme` onto `<html>` directly; (e) reload the page under each theme; (f) assert the resolved token values against a table of ratios measured once. | The override block at `desktop.scss` lines 1454-1459 exists only for the dark theme, so a single-theme case left it with no assertion and a later token change in dark would pass unnoticed; (a) documents that gap rather than closing it. One routine called twice keeps the two passes identical by construction, which (b) cannot promise as the probes evolve. `toggle_theme` xcalls `frappe.core.doctype.user.user.switch_theme` and would persist a theme onto the Administrator record — state the spec does not own — while `set_theme` is DOM-only, confirmed at runtime by the theme switch issuing zero network requests; it is also the entry point the Desk itself calls, which makes (d) a re-implementation of it. (e) would pay a second full dashboard load and widget fetch per theme. The background guard is what stops a dark pass from silently measuring the light palette if the attribute ever fails to take effect, a failure mode (f) is blind to because it never looks at what is painted. | Both passes run in one case, so its runtime roughly doubles (measured 5s) and a failure identifies its theme only through the assertion message. A run aborted mid-case leaves `data-theme="dark"` on that one page, which the next `cy.visit` discards. Only the two shipped themes are covered; a site that redefines the tokens still needs its own measurement (section 4.2). |
| RF-11 | The case measures the separator itself, reading `getComputedStyle(link, "::before").color` for every crumb whose `::before` renders content and asserting 4.5:1 against the painted background. | (a) Measure the anchor's own `color` only, as the case did before; (b) assert the separator's resolved token value; (c) skip the separator as decoration; (d) measure every crumb's `::before`, including the first one, whose `content` is `none`. | The separator carries its own colour declaration (RF-7), so the anchor's colour says nothing about it, and it is the surface D7's 4.17:1 breadcrumb figure actually matched once the link was raised. Asserting it is what makes RF-7 regression-proof: reverting the declaration to `--ink-gray-5`, rebuilding and re-running the case fails it at 4.174:1, which is how the probe was verified. (b) would prove the stylesheet rather than the painted colour, the error RF-9 records. (c) is the reading RF-7 rejects. (d) would measure a pseudo-element that paints nothing, since `.navbar-breadcrumbs li:first-child a:before` sets `content: none`, so the filter keeps the assertion about glyphs that are drawn. | The probe depends on `content` being reported as `none` where no glyph is drawn; a stylesheet that later draws the separator as a background image or an SVG would leave the probe measuring a colour nothing paints. |
| RF-12 | Re-declare `--focus-default` and `--focus-outline-default` on `body[data-route^="dashboard-view"]` as well (with a `[data-theme="dark"]` 3px variant), so the page chrome outside every widget on the dashboard route — the breadcrumb links and the page-head controls — resolves the raised ring colour. Geometry is unchanged: 2px light, 3px dark. | (a) Leave the raise scoped to `.dashboard-page, .widget-group, .widget`, as RF-1 shipped it; (b) raise `--focus-default` globally in `frappe/public/css/espresso/effects.css`; (c) add a literal `box-shadow` to `.navbar-breadcrumbs a:focus-visible` and the page-head buttons instead of re-scoping the token; (d) scope by a class on the dashboard container. | Under (a) the focused breadcrumb links and the page-head "Menu" button still resolved the shipped `#c9c9c9e5` / `#464646cc` — 1.566:1 light and 1.640:1 dark, which is the exact figure directive D7 names as the failure to repair, measured again on the running Desk after RF-1 shipped. Those controls live in the navbar and page head, outside any `.widget`, so no widget-scoped selector can reach them. (b) is what D7's containment clause forbids: the token has 13 consumers that paint every button, link, checkbox, datatable cell and filter in the Desk (RF-1). (c) would leave the token and the painted ring disagreeing on these surfaces and would miss any control added to the page head later. (d) fails for the same reason RF-6 records — the Desk `dashboard-view` page renders `<div class="dashboard">`, and `.dashboard-page` matches nothing on this route. The route attribute is the same hook RF-6 already uses for the breadcrumb colour, so the change reaches exactly one Desk route. | Every focusable control on `dashboard-view` routes — navbar, sidebar and page head included, not only the widgets — now paints the heavier `--ink-gray-6` ring. That is the intended outcome of the directive on this screen and it stops at this route: `/desk/build`, `/desk/todo` and `/desk/todo/new` keep the framework default, which the regression pass re-measured. A control that reads `--focus-default` to compute something other than a ring would read the raised value on this route. |
| RF-13 | Add `input`, `select`, `textarea` and `.form-control` to the `.widget { … :focus-visible }` ring list, and add one further rule that mirrors the `:not()` qualifiers of `frappe/public/scss/common/controls.scss`'s `.frappe-control:not([data-fieldtype="MultiSelectPills"]):not([data-fieldtype="Table MultiSelect"]).has-error input:focus` so a widget's control keeps the token ring while it also carries `has-error`. | (a) Add the four selectors only, without the mirrored rule; (b) add `!important` to the widget ring declaration; (c) rely on `:focus-visible` alone as the selector inside `.widget`; (d) remove the framework's error ring instead; (e) make the chart widget render the date-range control without `has-error`. | The list as shipped matched no form control, so the dashboard's only input painted bootstrap's `rgba(124,124,124,0.25)` ring at 1.342:1 with no error state and the framework's pale `var(--error-bg)` ring at 1.070:1 light / 1.799:1 dark with one — both far below the 3:1 this directive requires. (a) fixes only the first case: the error rule carries specificity (0,5,1) and sits later in the bundle than `desktop.scss` (`../common/controls` is imported after `desktop` in `frappe/public/scss/desk/index.scss`), so a (0,2,1) selector loses the cascade; the mirrored selector is (0,6,1) and wins on specificity alone, which is the same technique RD-10 used against bootstrap's corner reset. (b) wins by force and hides from either stylesheet why the rule exists. (c) would paint a ring on every focusable descendant of a widget, including containers a widget focuses programmatically. (d) would remove the error indication the framework gives every Desk input. (e) is the empty-render behaviour the lifecycle unit owns, and the ring fails at 1.342:1 even once the control renders without an error. | The error state's own 1px red border still paints while the control has focus, so the two states remain distinguishable, but the pale error ring no longer appears under keyboard focus inside a widget. The mirrored selector is coupled to the shape of the `controls.scss` rule: a future edit that drops one `:not()` qualifier would leave this rule matching nothing and the pale ring would return. Only keyboard focus (`:focus-visible`) is affected; a mouse click still shows the framework's own focus styling. |
| RF-14 | Raise the applied option of a widget dropdown with `.widget .dropdown-item.active, .widget .dropdown-item.active:hover { background-color: var(--ink-gray-8); color: var(--fg-color) }`. | (a) Leave it on bootstrap's `$component-active-bg`; (b) darken the background to `var(--ink-gray-7)`; (c) change `$component-active-bg` in `frappe/public/scss/desk/variables.scss`; (d) change only the text colour; (e) mark the applied option with an icon or a border instead of a filled pill. | `$component-active-bg` is the Sass literal `$gray-600` (`#7c7c7c`), so the pill is the same mid grey in both themes and measures 4.174:1 light and 4.295:1 dark against a 4.5:1 text requirement — the surface RD-3 handed to this directive, and the one item a viewer must read to know which window is applied. `--ink-gray-8` inverts with the theme (`#171717` light, `#d9d9d9` dark) and `--fg-color`, which bootstrap already paints the label with, inverts with it, so one background swap gives 17.928:1 light and 12.701:1 dark — both measured on the running Desk. (b) also passes (11.726:1 / 8.173:1) but leaves less margin and a paler pill in dark. (c) is a global change: `$component-active-bg` is bootstrap's active colour for every dropdown, nav pill and list-group item in the Desk, which D7's containment clause rules out. (d) cannot reach 4.5:1 on `#7c7c7c` with any text colour available. (e) would change the marking pattern RD-3 chose and leave the pill's own contrast unfixed. | The applied option is now a near-black pill in light and a near-white pill in dark, a heavier treatment than bootstrap's grey. The rule is scoped to `.widget`, so every other Desk dropdown keeps the shipped pill — a deliberate inconsistency between widget menus and the rest of the Desk, and the price of the containment clause. Text colour is pinned in the same rule, so the pair cannot drift if bootstrap's `$dropdown-link-active-color` changes. |
| RF-15 | Paint the chart's over-bar value labels with `.dashboard-widget-box .frappe-chart text.data-point-value { fill: var(--text-color) }`. | (a) Leave them as they are; (b) set `fill` from `--charts-label-color`, the token frappe-charts uses for axis text; (c) pass a colour into the chart options from `frappe/public/js/frappe/utils/utils.js::make_chart`; (d) turn `show_values_over_chart` off on the Top Owners chart record; (e) add a contrasting halo or background plate behind each label. | frappe-charts creates those `<text>` nodes with no `fill` attribute, no inline style and no rule of its own, so they take the SVG initial black: 21:1 in light and **1.17:1** on the dark card — the worst reading anywhere on the dashboard, and invisible to Lighthouse because axe-core does not evaluate SVG `<text>` contrast at all. `--text-color` is the Desk body text token and inverts with the theme, giving 17.928:1 light and 12.701:1 dark, measured from the painted `fill` on the running Desk. (b) resolves to a muted grey chosen for axis ticks, not for a data value a viewer reads. (c) would change a shared chart helper for one chart's labels and would have to be threaded through every caller. (d) removes information the AAP's record asks for (`show_values_over_chart: 1`, §0.6.1). (e) would add markup frappe-charts owns. | The rule is scoped to dashboard chart widgets, so a frappe-charts instance rendered anywhere else in the Desk keeps the library's black labels. It targets the library's class name, so a future frappe-charts release that renames `data-point-value` or starts setting its own `fill` would silently take the rule out of effect — the label colour is worth re-measuring on any chart-library upgrade. |
| RF-16 | Suppress the breadcrumb `/` separator on dashboard-view routes at the widths where only one crumb is rendered: `@media (max-width: 991px) { body[data-route^="dashboard-view"] .navbar-breadcrumbs a:before { display: none } }`. | (a) Drive the suppression from the same media query for every Desk route, in `frappe/public/scss/desk/mobile.scss`; (b) re-evaluate `frappe.is_mobile()` on resize so `frappe/public/js/frappe/ui/page.html`'s baked `mobile-no-divider` class stays correct; (c) hide the crumb's `::before` only when it is `li:last-child`; (d) raise the `mobile.scss` crumb-hiding query from 991px to the 768px the shell's class uses; (e) leave the artefact, since it predates this work. | `page.html` bakes `mobile-no-divider` once at shell build from a memoised `is_mobile()` (`< 768`), while `mobile.scss` hides every crumb but the last at `<= 991px`, so the surviving crumb kept a leading "/" between 768 and 991px and at any width reached by resizing down from >= 768px. RF-7 raised that glyph from `--ink-gray-4` to `--ink-gray-6`, which made a pre-existing artefact prominent, so this directive is where it is answered. A media query is live at every width regardless of the load path, which is what (b) cannot promise without changing shared shell behaviour, and it needs no JavaScript. (a) and (d) change the breadcrumb of every Desk route and page type — list, form, workspace, report — which D7's containment clause and the checkpoint's "list and form pages unchanged" expectation both rule out; (d) would additionally re-show the crumbs it hides between 768 and 991px, a layout change on every narrow Desk screen. (c) is narrower than needed and would not cover a trail whose last crumb is hidden by some future rule. (e) leaves a dangling glyph that assistive technology reads into the crumb's accessible name. | The separator is suppressed for every crumb on this route below 992px, so a dashboard trail deeper than the two crumbs this feature renders would show its crumbs without separators at those widths — no such trail exists on the `dashboard-view` page. Every other Desk route keeps the artefact, which stays open for whoever owns the shell. |
| RF-17 | Restore the breadcrumb hover colour on this route with `body[data-route^="dashboard-view"] .navbar-breadcrumbs a:hover { color: var(--ink-gray-8) }`. | (a) Leave hover as RF-6 left it; (b) lower RF-6's specificity so `breadcrumb.scss`'s `:hover` wins again; (c) use `var(--ink-gray-7)`, the value `breadcrumb.scss` hovers to; (d) express the hover state with an underline or a background instead of a colour change. | RF-6's selector is (0,2,2) and out-specifies `.navbar-breadcrumbs a:hover` (0,2,1), so on this route a hovered crumb painted the same `--ink-gray-6` as an idle one: the interactive feedback every other Desk route gives was lost, which is a regression introduced by this refinement rather than a pre-existing gap. (b) would give up the contrast raise that D7 asks for. (c) is the framework's own hover value but only one step from the raised base (`#383838` vs `#525252` light, and `#afafaf` vs `#999999` dark), so the change would be hard to perceive; `--ink-gray-8` is the next token and measures 17.928:1 light / 12.701:1 dark, a clear luminance jump from the 7.814:1 / 6.293:1 base. (d) would introduce a hover treatment the Desk breadcrumb does not use anywhere else. | Hovering a crumb on this route darkens it further than on other Desk routes, where hover stops at `--ink-gray-7`. The `::before` separator keeps the base colour on hover, which is what `breadcrumb.scss` does too. The current-page crumb keeps `--ink-gray-9 !important` from `li:last-child > a` and is unaffected. |
| RF-18 | Add `box-shadow: var(--focus-default)` to the existing `.widget.shortcut-widget-box:focus-visible` rule. | (a) Leave the tile as it was; (b) broaden the contrast block to `.widget:focus-visible, .widget [tabindex]:focus-visible`; (c) remove the rule's `outline: 0` so the browser's native ring paints; (d) raise the contrast of the tile's focus background instead of adding a ring. | The block RF-1 added is a descendant selector (`.widget [tabindex]:focus-visible`), so a tile that *is* the `.widget` resolved the raised token and never consumed it: its only focus rule set an underline plus a `--highlight-color` fill measuring 1.062:1 light / 1.088:1 dark and zeroed the outline, leaving no indicator of the 3:1 the directive requires. (c) gives a ring that ignores the Desk token, the inconsistency RE-8 records. | RF-19 | Give the chart error state's Retry control a resting boundary: `border: 1px solid var(--ink-gray-6)`, declared on `.chart-retry` inside the `.dashboard-widget-box .chart-loading-state` block, with RC-7's `:focus-visible` ring nested inside that rule. | (a) Leave the control as it was; (b) use `var(--border-color)`, the Desk's own card border token; (c) use `var(--ink-gray-5)`; (d) give the button a filled `btn-primary`-style background instead of a border; (e) keep two sibling rules for `.chart-retry` rather than nesting the focus rule inside one. | At rest the button's `--btn-default-bg` fill (`#f3f3f3` light, `#292929` dark) sits on the error panel's `--subtle-accent` (`#f8f8f8`, `#1f1f1f`) at about 1.04:1 and 1.13:1 with `border-width: 0` and no shadow, so only its glyphs read as a control — below WCAG 2.2 SC 1.4.11's 3:1 for a control's visual boundary. `--ink-gray-6` is the token RF-2 already chose for indicators on these surfaces and measures 7.042:1 against the button's own fill and 7.358:1 against the panel in light, 5.106:1 and 5.785:1 in dark. (d) would make one recovery control the most prominent element on the dashboard. | RF-20 | Paint the breadcrumb focus ring inside the crumb's own box on dashboard-view routes: `box-shadow: none; outline: var(--focus-outline-default); outline-offset: -2px` (and `-3px` under `[data-theme="dark"]`, matching that theme's 3px token width). | (a) Stop at RF-12's token raise; (b) set `overflow: visible` on the `.ellipsis` ancestors so the outset shadow is not clipped; (c) use an inset `box-shadow` with a literal colour; (d) give the crumbs a background-colour change on focus instead of a ring; (e) leave the outset `box-shadow` in place beside the inset outline. | RF-12 raised the token, and the crumbs resolved it — but `a:focus-visible` in `frappe/public/scss/common/buttons.scss` paints it as an outset `box-shadow`, and the crumb's `li.ellipsis`, `ul.navbar-breadcrumbs.ellipsis` and `div.title-area.ellipsis` ancestors are all `overflow: hidden` with the same bounds as the anchor, so the paint was discarded: pixel sampling of the running Desk found **zero** ring pixels on the first crumb and a 2-3px sliver at the left edge of the second, in both themes.
